<heading><span class="col_I1" refersTo="page_0009"/>INDEX TO SUBJECTS.</heading>

<p>In this index &#x201C;R.&#x201D; denotes &#x201C;Reading.&#x201D;</p>

<p>The sign &#x2020; indicates that the Bill or other matter concerned dropped owing to theprorogation of Parliament.</p>

<p>Matters which have been given headings in this index may also form the subject of questions, and will be found separately indexed under the headings &#x201C;Questions&#x201D; or &#x201C;Oral Questions.&#x201D;</p>

<li>Unparliamentary language&#x2014;

<ul>

<li>Expressions challenged&#x2014;

<ul>

<li>&#x201C;That is a lie&#x201D;, 122, 161; &#x201C;At that time he took more interest in getting the bankrupt estates of farmers than working in their real interests&#x201D;, 4899.</li></ul></li>

<li>Expressions ruled out of order (*and ordered to be withdrawn)&#x2014;

<ul>

<li>&#x201C;That is a lie&#x201D;, 122, 161; *&#x201C;the same cowardly insinuations which you had made before&#x201D;, 324; *&#x201C;He sold his Afrikaners for 1s. a piece&#x201D;, 351; *&#x201C;the impression he (an honourable member) gave me was that of a bush tick on an ox&#x2019;s tail which imagines it can swallow up the ox&#x201D;, 1535; *&#x201C;the hon. member repeats what he must know is a lie&#x201D;, 1448-9; *&#x201C;they (honourable members) are not told to show up his (the Prime Minister&#x2019;s) lies&#x201D;, 1456-7; *&#x201C;an infamous statement&#x201D;, 1461; *&#x201C;what the hon. member is saying is a &#x2018;public lie&#x2019;&#x201D;, 1516; *&#x201C;hon. members opposite are busy protecting themselves&#x201D;, 1577; *&#x201C;It is a deliberate lie&#x201D;, 2402; *&#x201C;that is a lie&#x201D;, 2532; *&#x201C;He is deliberately misrepresenting the position&#x201D;, 2877; *&#x201C;The Leader of the Opposition knew that it was untrue&#x201D;, 3057; *&#x201C;what the hon. member has stated here is nothing but a slanderous lie&#x201D;, 3092; *&#x201C;No one knows better than he that that is an untruth&#x201D;, 3708; *&#x201C;The Minister has been deliberately untruthful&#x201D;, 3970; *&#x201C;I am going to say that the hon. member says things which he knows are not true&#x201D;, 4451; &#x201C;scurrilous innuendo&#x201D;, 5443; *&#x201C;I thought he was a gentleman; apparently we were both mistaken&#x201D;, 5444; *&#x201C;Is a Minister of the Crown privileged to lie in private but not in public&#x201D;, 5445; *&#x201C;the Prime Minister was desirous of protecting the Minister of Labour from his own folly and gross incompetence&#x201D;, 5446; *&#x201C;the hon. member was very dexterous in teaching natives to impersonate and vote on behalf of those who were dead&#x201D;, 7092.</li>

<ul>

<li>&#x201C;That is a lie,&#x201D; 122, 161; *&#x201C;the same cowardly insinuations which you had made before,&#x201D; 324; *&#x201C;He sold his Afrikaners for 1s. a piece,&#x201D; 351; *&#x201C;the impression he (an honourable member) gave me was that of a bush tick on an ox&#x2019;s tail which imagines it can swallow up the ox,&#x201D; 1436; *&#x201C;the hon. member repeats what he must know is a lie,&#x201D; 1448-9; *&#x201C;they (honourable members) are not told to show up his (the Prime Minister&#x2019;s) lies,&#x201D; 1456-7; *&#x201C;an infamous statement,&#x201D; 1461; *&#x201C;what the hon. member is saying is a &#x2018;public lie&#x2019;,&#x201D; 1516; *&#x201C;hon. members opposite are busy protecting themselves,&#x201D; 1577.</li></ul></li>

<p class="heading">HOUSE OF ASSEMBLY DEBATES</p>
